宏观经济点评

体增速。
投资再度走弱。10 月，固定资产投资累计同比下降 8.1%，降幅较前三季
度扩大 0.9 个百分点。按照累计投资额和累计增速粗略反推，10 月单月固
定资产投资同比降幅约为 16.9%，9 月的单月企稳并未延续。扣除房地产
后的固定资产投资累计下降约 5.1%，降幅也较前三季度扩大约 0.9 个百
分点；民间投资下降 11.4%，降幅扩大 1.0 个百分点，指向投资弱化不只
是地产问题。
制造业投资小幅修复，增长仍集中在少数优势领域。10 月，制造业累计
投资下降 2.9%，降幅较前三季度扩大 0.8 个百分点。国家统计局目前公
布的 13 个制造业行业中，仅纺织业、铁路船舶航空航天和计算机通信电
子设备制造业投资保持正增长；正增长行业数量由前三季度的 5 个减少至
3 个。具体看，一方面，优势产业仍然具备需求、生产和资本开支相互支
撑的条件。更宽口径的高技术产业投资增长 4.0%，其中信息服务、航空
航天设备、电子及通信设备投资分别增长 20.5%、10.2%和 6.6%。另一

方面，部分上游原料料行业和下游制造业投资维持负增。农副食品、食
品、化工、医药、金属制品、专用设备和汽车制造业投资分别下降
4.1%、10.7%、8.4%、9.2%、9.9%、9.0%和 7.9%。
地产投资降幅扩大，现房销售制度下销售回款向开发投资传导节奏出现变
化。10 月，地产投资累计同比下降 21.6%，降幅较前三季度扩大 1.0 个
百分点；按累计金额反推，10 月单月投资下降约 33.2%，较 9 月约
26.3%的降幅进一步扩大。结构上看，一方面， 地产销售存在局部企
稳。销售端看，10 月，单月商品房销售面积和销售额分别下降约 19.1%
和 18.6%，降幅较 9 月分别收窄约 1.6 个和 1.5 个百分点；二手房交易网
签面积累计增长 11.4%。价格端看，10 月，一、二、三线城市新房价格
同比降幅分别收窄 0.4 个、0.4 个和 0.3 个百分点，二手房价格同比降幅
也均有收窄；环比看，一线城市新房价格环比由上涨转为持平，二线城市
由持平转为下降，或指向价格整体修复动能不足。另一方面，现房销售制

度改革背景下销售回款对投资的前置融资功能减弱。资金端看，1—10 月
房地产开发企业到位资金下降 21.9%，降幅较前三季度扩大 0.3 个百分
点。其中，国内贷款和自筹资金分别下降 33.4%和 21.9%，定金及预收款
下降 15.5%，个人按揭贷款下降 22.1%。到位资金降幅明显大于商品房销
售额 14.3%的累计降幅，一定程度上表明销售收入未同步转化为开发企业
可以用于投资的有效资金。供给端看，1—10 月房屋施工面积下降
13.0%，竣工面积下降 26.6%，降幅较前三季度分别扩大 0.2 个和 1.5 个
百分点；新开工面积下降 24.4%，降幅小幅收窄，仍处较低水平。
基建延续回落，资金尚未转化为实物工作量。10 月，基建投资累计同比
下降 5.1%，降幅较前三季度扩大 0.9 个百分点。其中，电热燃水投资下
降 7.7%，交通运输仓储邮政投资增长 0.7%，水利环境和公共设施管理投
资下降 14.4%，均较前三季度有所放缓。我们分析，政策资金到项目建设
存在传导时滞是一个重要原因。截至 10 月 28 日，8000 亿元新型政策性

### Page 3

宏观经济点评  
请务必阅读正文之后的信息披露和法律声明 3 
内部尚存在结构性支撑。1—10 月信息传输业、航空运输业和水上运输业
投资分别增长 32.0%、12.5%和 12.5%，数字基础设施及部分交通网络仍
保持较快增长。 
商品消费再度转弱，服务消费提供相对支撑。9 月，社零同比增长
0.4%，较 9 月下降 0.7 个百分点，环比增长 0.02%。其中，商品和餐饮
消费均有所放缓，分别增长 0.3%和 0.7%，较 9 月分别下降 0.7 个和 1.4
个百分点。结构上看，一方面，商品消费走弱范围扩大。10 月汽车类零
售额下降 21.4%，降幅与 9 月基本持平；剔除汽车后，消费品零售额增长
2.5%，较 9 月下降 1.4 个百分点。9 月相对较强的通讯器材类零售增速由
31.1%降至 6.5%，服装和日用品分别下降 2.0%和 0.4%，金银珠宝下降
28.7%，建筑装潢材料下降 11.2%。另一方面，服务消费仍具有相对韧
性。1—10 月服务零售额增长 4.9%，高于商品零售额的 0.9%；网上服务
零售额增长 5.0%，快于网上商品零售额的 2.9%。通讯信息服务、旅游咨
询租赁和文体休闲服务增长较快，指向服务消费较商品消费更具韧性。 
往后看，未来经济的增量支撑仍主要来自两条线索。一是投资端的政策重
点正由资金投放转向项目实施和实物工作量形成。截至 10 月 28 日，
8000 亿元新型政策性金融工具已经完成投放；11 月 6 日国常会进一步提
出加快重大项目和“六张网”建设实施进度，用好用足各项政策性资金，
及时足额拨付涉及基层和企业的各类款项，并支持企业扩大设备更新和无
形资产投资，指向当前政策正在着力打通资金拨付和项目施工之间的堵
点。二是消费政策由商品补贴向服务供给和消费基础设施延伸。国常会提
出加强消费线上线下、城乡联动，畅通投资与消费良性循环，叠加此前消
费基础设施建设政策对文旅、体育、养老托育、家政和健康等领域的部
署，我们预计服务消费仍会快于商品消费。

风险提示：实物工作量形成不及预期、AI 产业链超预期降温、外需边际走
弱。

宏观经济点评  
请务必阅读正文之后的信息披露和法律声明 6 
1. 生产：工业生产增速回落，外需支撑优势制造业高增 
工业生产回落。10 月，规上工业增加值同比增长 4.8%，较 9 月下降 0.7 个百分点，
环比增长 0.2%，低于季节性。分门类看，采矿业、制造业和电热燃水业增加值分别增长
0.7%、5.4%和 4.4%，较 9 月分别下降 0.6 个、0.6 个和 1.2 个百分点，工业生产呈现较
为广泛的降温。我们分析工业生产放缓主要有两方面拖累，一是地产、基建及部分传统
制造业需求继续走弱，生产改善不具备向更广泛行业扩散的条件。10 月，非金制品、黑
金冶炼和有色冶炼增加值分别下降 2.3%、1.7%和 4.4%；钢材、水泥、平板玻璃和十种
有色金属产量分别下降 5.1%、1.3%、16.0%和 1.2%。化工和橡胶塑料制品增加值也仅
增长 1.3%和 1.2%。汽车制造业增加值增速由 6.7%降至 0.9%，汽车产量下降 7.7%。
二是国际能源价格再次上涨强化中下游制造业成本约束，可能对部分行业扩产形成压制。
10 月布伦特原油现货均价升至每桶 124 美元，较 9 月上涨约 10 美元。同期工业生产者
购进价格同比上涨 7.9%，高于出厂价格 5.7%的涨幅，其中燃料动力类和化工原料类购
进价格分别上涨 18.6%和 16.8%；制造业 PMI 原材料购进价格指数为 56.8%，仍处于
较高扩张区间。从生产数据看，10 月原油加工量下降 8.1%，化工、橡胶塑料等成本敏
感行业增加值仅低位增长。我们分析，油价上涨未必是工业生产回落的直接原因，但叠
加内需偏弱、成本传导不畅，可能会放大成本约束，挤压部分中下游企业的扩产空间。  
工业生产主要增量依旧集中于外需强劲背景下的优势制造领域。10 月，规模以上工
业企业出口交货值增长 18.6%，较 9 月提高 1.6 个百分点；装备制造业出口交货值增长
20.6%，其中汽车、电气机械和电子行业分别增长 52.2%、23.7%和 15.3%，三者对全
部工业出口交货值增长的贡献率合计达到 66.7%。与出口结构相对应，高技术制造业和
装备制造业增加值分别增长 17.6%和 10.6%，计算机通信电子设备、专用设备、铁路船
舶航空航天设备制造业分别增长 19.4%、11.5%和 9.2%；集成电路、工业机器人和民用
钢质船舶产量分别增长 21.9%、26.0%和 35.6%。 
服务业小幅放缓，生产性和数字化服务仍是主要支撑。10 月，服务业生产指数同比
增长 4.6%，较 9 月下降 0.2 个百分点；服务业商务活动指数为 50.0%，较 9 月下降 0.2
个百分点。结构上看，生产性和数字化服务增速较高。租赁和商务服务业、信息传输软
件和信息技术服务业、交通运输仓储和邮政业生产指数分别增长 9.3%、9.2%和 5.1%，
均高于服务业整体增速。

2. 投资：延续回落 
投资再度走弱。10 月，固定资产投资累计同比下降 8.1%，降幅较前三季度扩大 0.9
个百分点。按照累计投资额和累计增速粗略反推，10 月单月固定资产投资同比降幅约为
16.9%，9 月的单月企稳并未延续。扣除房地产后的固定资产投资累计下降约 5.1%，降
幅也较前三季度扩大约 0.9 个百分点；民间投资下降 11.4%，降幅扩大 1.0 个百分点，
指向投资弱化不只是地产问题。

制造业投资小幅修复，增长仍集中在少数优势领域。10 月，制造业累计投资下降
2.9%，降幅较前三季度扩大 0.8 个百分点。国家统计局目前公布的 13 个制造业行业中，

3. 消费：再度转弱 
商品消费再度转弱，服务消费提供相对支撑。9 月，社零同比增长 0.4%，较 9 月下
降 0.7 个百分点，环比增长 0.02%。其中，商品和餐饮消费均有所放缓，分别增长 0.3%
和 0.7%，较 9 月分别下降 0.7 个和 1.4 个百分点。结构上看，一方面，商品消费走弱范
围扩大。10 月汽车类零售额下降 21.4%，降幅与 9 月基本持平；剔除汽车后，消费品零
售额增长 2.5%，较 9 月下降 1.4 个百分点。9 月相对较强的通讯器材类零售增速由 31.1%
降至 6.5%，服装和日用品分别下降 2.0%和 0.4%，金银珠宝下降 28.7%，建筑装潢材
料下降 11.2%。另一方面，服务消费仍具有相对韧性。1—10 月服务零售额增长 4.9%，
高于商品零售额的 0.9%；网上服务零售额增长 5.0%，快于网上商品零售额的 2.9%。通
讯信息服务、旅游咨询租赁和文体休闲服务增长较快，指向服务消费较商品消费更具韧
性。

4. 展望：实物工作量形成是重点 
往后看，未来经济的增量支撑仍主要来自两条线索。一是投资端的政策重点正由资
金投放转向项目实施和实物工作量形成。截至 10 月 28 日，8000 亿元新型政策性金融
工具已经完成投放；11 月 6 日国常会进一步提出加快重大项目和“六张网”建设实施进
度，用好用足各项政策性资金，及时足额拨付涉及基层和企业的各类款项，并支持企业
扩大设备更新和无形资产投资，指向当前政策正在着力打通资金拨付和项目施工之间的
堵点。二是消费政策由商品补贴向服务供给和消费基础设施延伸。国常会提出加强消费
线上线下、城乡联动，畅通投资与消费良性循环，叠加此前消费基础设施建设政策对文
旅、体育、养老托育、家政和健康等领域的部署，我们预计服务消费仍会快于商品消费。

评级标准为报告发布日后的 6 个月内公司股价（或行业
指数）相对同期基准指数的相对市场表现。其中 A 股市
场以沪深 300 指数为基准；新三板市场以三板成指（针
对协议转让标的）或三板做市指数（针对做市转让标的）
为基准；香港市场以恒生指数为基准。 
股票评级 
买入 相对同期基准指数涨幅在 10%以上 
增持 相对同期基准指数涨幅在 5%~10%之间 
中性 相对同期基准指数涨幅在-5%~+5%之间 
减持 相对同期基准指数跌幅在 5%以上 
行业评级 
看好 相对表现优于市场 
中性 相对表现与市场持平 
看淡 相对表现弱于市场

## Document 026: 2026-10-27_［門間一夫の経済深読み］成長戦略より21世紀ニッポンの「アポロ計画」を_218ba051

Source PDF: all report pdf/2026-10-27_［門間一夫の経済深読み］成長戦略より21世紀ニッポンの「アポロ計画」を_218ba051.pdf
Report source: Mizuho
Date: 2026-10-27
Pages: 4
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

1

門間 一夫の 
経済深読み

成長戦略より 21 世紀ニッポンの「 ア
ポロ計画」を 
みずほ総合研究所  
エグゼクティブエコノミスト  門間一夫 2026 年 10 月 27 日

心情的にはノーサレンダー路線 
先月の論考では、①度重なる成長戦略にもかかわらず日本の潜在成長率は低下したこと、②成長戦略
には政策手段の特定が困難という基本問題があるこ と 、③ 25 年英知を尽くしても結果が出なかった事実
は厳粛に受け止めるべきこと、などを述べた 1。 
それを踏まえ、本稿ではあるべき方向性を考えてみたい。選択肢は、(a)25 年で諦めず 50 年でも 100
年でもやる、(b)成長目標を達成しやすい水準に下げる、(c)政府のミッションは成長戦略とは別にある
と考える、の 3 つである。選択肢(b)が無難に見えるが、もともと成長戦略は「現状の成長率ではだめ
だ」という認識に基づくものである。現状を明確に上回る成長率を目指さない限り、成長戦略としては
自己矛盾である。 
すると残るのは (a)ノーサレンダー路線、(c)ミッション再考、のいずれかである。このうちノーサレ
ンダー路線、つまり「成長を諦めない」という不屈の姿勢には人々の共感が得られやすい。しかし、不
屈の志を掲げても、手段の裏付けを欠くという現実は変わらない。手段の裏付けを欠くなら「目標」で
はなく「夢」である。もちろん、夢を語ることが悪いわけではないし、政治家は夢を語るべきという考
えもあるだろう。その夢が偶然にでもかなえば歴史に残る偉業であり、夢なら実現せずとも落胆の度合
いは小さい。ノーサレンダー路線はアップサイドだけでダウンサイドはない、と考えてよいだろうか。 
成長戦略のダウンサイド 
成長戦略にダウンサイドはない、やってみて損はない、と い う 前提は実は正しくない。成長戦略は「た
だ」でできるわけではないからだ。例えば、成長戦略には多かれ少なかれネット財政支出が伴う。国債
をどれだけ発行できるのか議論の余地はあるが、いずれにせよ有限である。 
一方、成長戦略は総花的になりやすい。成長を実現する手段がはっきりしない以上、ある政策案を「 成
長とは無関係」ときっぱり排除するのも難しいからである。経済界の要望や政治家の信念に基づく様々
な政策案が、成長に少しでも関係しそうなら幅広く正当化される。各種施策の費用対効果について一定

1 門間一夫の経済深読み「成長戦略の本質的な限界」（2026 年 9 月 25 日 ）。

### Page 2

2

の分析や議論は行われている。しかし、行われることとそれが有効に機能することは別である。費用対
効果の分析が有効に機能し、各種施策の経済成長へのインパクトが正確に測れていたとしたら、25 年に
わたり成長目標の大幅未達という結果にはならなかったはずである。 
一般に、政策目標の実現に向けてリソースが投入され、目標が実現できなかった場合、そのリソース
投入は失敗だったことになる。チャレンジ自体は無駄ではなかったと言える場合もあるが、過去 25 年
だめだった目標にもう一度挑戦してみるという話なら、たぶん次もだめだろうと事前に予想できる。金
も人もふんだんにあるなら別だが、財政状況が厳しく、人材の不足も叫ばれる国で、高確率で失敗する
ミッションへのリソース投入は賢明な選択と言えるのか。 
有限のリソースが無駄に使われれば、他の課題のために使えるリソースが減る。ここで言うリソース
は財政資金だけではない。実現できない目的のために、多忙な官僚の労力が割かれ、政治家のポリティ
カル・キャピタルも消耗すれば、やればできたはずの他の課題への対応まで中途半端に終わってしまう。
成長戦略には他の課題を犠牲にするという「機会費用」が伴うのであり、それが「ただではない」とい
うことのより正確な意味である。「成長を諦めないノーサレンダー路線でいく」という選択は、「50 年で
も 100 年でもその機会費用を払い続けることを覚悟する」という選択でもある。 
何のための経済成長か 
ここで話を一段階前に戻してみよう。そもそもなぜ、経済成長を高めるべきと考えられているのだろ
うか。もちろん、低成長より高成長の方がいいに決まっているので、経済成長を簡単に高められるので
あれば、そんな疑問を持つ必要はない。しかし、経済成長を高めようとすることで機会費用を払い続け
ることになる確率が高いなら、そうまでしてわれわれが最終的に望んでいるものは何なのかという問い
に、きちんと向き合う必要がある。 
経済成長の必要性を訴える主張は、「〇〇の解決には経済成長が不可欠」「低成長のままなら〇〇のよ
うな不都合が起きる」というパターンにおおむね当てはまるように思う。つまり、現在直面している課
題、あるいは将来直面しそうな課題に対応するには、より高い経済成長が必要という認識が成長戦略の
基礎にあると考えられる。念頭に置かれている課題の中身は、社会保障や財政の持続性、行政・医療・
介護サービスの維持、国際的な存在感の回復、生活水準の向上など、論者により様々である。パイを増
やせるなら改善できる課題は多いので、経済成長を高めるべきという主張は万人受けし、政治家には格
好の政策アジェンダになる。 
しかし、「何らかの課題を経済成長で解決する」という考え方は 、「 その課題に直接向き合うより経済
成長を高める方が簡単だ」という前提がないと成立しない。その前提が間違っていれば、むしろ経済成
長に頼るがゆえに肝心の課題がいつまでも解決しないことになる。重要な課題であればあるほど、経済
成長と切り離して解決する努力を早く始めるべきではないか。 
例えば、社会保障や財政の持続性が課題なのであれば、給付と負担のバランスや、恵まれている人と
そうでない人の公平感を改善することで、問題の解決を目指す方が良い。人々の生活水準を高めたいと
いう課題を経済成長と切り離すのは少し厄介だが、国民全員が等しく困っているわけではなく、問題の

### Page 3

3

本質は経済格差にある。分配政策により、国民生活の満足感や社会の安定性を全体として改善すること
はできる。 
国際社会における日本の存在感を高めることは、経済成長ではそもそも無理であろう。かつて日本が
世界第 2 位の GDP をキープし、経済大国として存在感を示した時代もあった。しかし、あれは冷戦構造
のもとでの日米関係、戦後の高度成長とその余熱、新興国が発展初期段階にあったことなどによる歴史
の偶然であった。GDP の対世界シェアが 3～4％程度まで落ち、今後さらに人口が減り、資源・エネルギ
ーに乏しく、特殊な言語を使う島国が、今から経済成長を多少高められたとしても、そのことで示せる
国際パワーは高が知れている。小さくても存在感がある国をどう作るかが、正しいアジェンダ設定だと
思われる。 
21 世紀ニッポンの「アポロ計画」は？ 
日本が課題だらけなのは確かだが、25 年間どうやっても達成できなかった経済成長に、その解決をこ
れ以上頼るのが正解とは思いにくい。逆に、優先課題の解決に国が本気でリソースを投入するなら、そ
こに民間も収益機会を見出し、様々な連鎖反応を通じて経済に好影響が及ぶ可能性も出てくる。 
経済学者マリアナ・マッツカートは著書「ミッション・エコノミー」において、大きな変革は市場メ
カニズムだけでは生み出せず、国家が野心的だが実行可能な課題を設定し、民間の力を引き出す仕組み
を築くことが重要と説く 2。マッツカートは代表例として、1960 年代の米国アポロ計画を挙げる。1969
年 7 月 20 日、ニール・アームストロングとバズ・オルドリンが月面に降り立ったことで知られる、あの
アポロ計画である。 
日本の成長戦略も「野心的な課題」を掲げたものではあるが、複雑すぎるマクロ経済そのものを課題
の対象にすると、先月の論考で詳しく述べたように、目的と手段の関係性が特定できない。原因と結果
の関係がよくわからない仕事は、責任の所在があいまいで互いに人任せになり、結果にコミットするこ
との効果が得られない。それに対し、「月に人を送る」などのより具体的なミッションの場合、そのため
には何が必要なのかの因果関係を分析・認識しやすい分、結果にコミットすれば人々の力が正しい方向
へ向かう。 
アポロ計画は、半導体、コンピューター、通信、材料工学などの技術を飛躍的に発展させ、その後の
米国の産業基盤を築いたと言われている。また、計画推進の国家機関であった米航空宇宙局（NASA）は 、
多くの科学技術人材を育成・輩出した。彼らはその後、金融イノベーションなどでも活躍し、NASA 出身
なので「ロケット・サイエンティスト」と呼ばれたことはよく知られている。 
ここで重要なのは、半導体やコンピューターなどの産業育成自体を目的として、政府が補助金で支援
したという話ではないことである。アポロ計画という具体的なミッションがまず存在し、それへの貢献
に適正な対価で報いる仕組みがイノベーターたちを突き動かし、様々な技術や知見が開発、蓄積された
のである。後世に残った産業基盤や人材は、「月に人を送る」というミッションの副産物であった。

2 Mazzucato, Mariana(2021) Mission Economy: A Moonshot Guide to Changing Capitalism, Allen Lane-Penguin 
（関美和・鈴木絵里子訳『ミッション・エコノミー』News Picks、2021 年 ）。

### Page 4

4

一般論として、何らかの課題を解決すれば、経済に必ず大きなプラスの効果が見込める、とまでは言
えない。しかし、上位目標が「課題の解決」なのだから、それが達成された時点でミッションは成功で
あり、投入されたリソースは「有効に使われた」ことになる。そのうえで、マクロ経済にもプラスの波
及効果があればなお良いが、仮にそれがなくても課題が解決された分、以前より望ましい社会になって
いる。 
最大の難関は、優先課題を特定しそれに取り組む社会的モメンタムを生み出すことであり、それには
既存の発想に捉われない強力なビジョンとリーダーシップが要る。アポロ計画にも当初「無謀で財政資
金の無駄使い」という声があった。マッツカートは先ほどの著書の中で、仮に現代風の費用対効果分析
を適用していたら、アポロ計画は採用されず、人類が月面に立つことはなかったと言っている。 
アポロ計画に相当する案件は、どの時代、どの国にも自明なものとしてあるわけではない。「月に人を
送る」というミッションは、当時の米ソ冷戦状況において、ソ連に宇宙開発で打ち勝つことが米国の死
活問題、という歴史特殊的な認識のもとで生まれた。それでも、よく考えてみれば今の日本にも、人口
の減少・高齢化や地政学環境の激変など、人類史上にもまれな歴史特殊的な条件のもとで、「死活問題」
になりつつある課題はいくつかあるように思う。例えば、エネルギーや食料の安定供給、生活インフラ・
医療・介護体制の維持・改善などは、次世代の日本人にとってまさに「死活問題」なのではないか。 
限られたリソースの優先課題への配分が、市場メカニズムで実現できないなら、国が強力に関与しな
ければならない。民間には不得意な国民的課題の解決こそ、国の本来の役割であり、そのためのリソー
スを国債発行でまかないきれないなら、国民に追加負担を求めてもいい。 
言うまでもなくこれは困難な作業である。だから優先課題は何か、そのために国民負担を求めるべき
かなどを深く議論せず、経済成長で様々な問題をまとめて解決できることにしておく方が、実はずっと
楽なのである。しかし、それが問題の先送りにすぎないことは繰り返し述べたとおりである。今も成長
戦略が重要だと言い、政府にそれを求めたがるわれわれの意識は、日本が直面する真の課題やその優先
順位を考えるのは面倒だ、という思考停止から生まれているのかもしれない。21 世紀ニッポンの「アポ
ロ計画」は何なのか、国民的な議論の活性化が必要ではないだろうか。

© 2026 株式会社みずほ銀行

MSCI 降評風險升高，印尼股市保守看待
2026 年 10 月 27 日
一、 市場動態
1. MSCI 擬將印尼降至獨立市場，降評風險明顯升高：MSCI 於 10 月 15 日宣布，啟動
將印尼股市由「新興市場（Emerging Market）」 直接降至「獨立市場（Standalone
Market）」的諮詢程序，意見徵詢將於 2027 年 4 月 30 日截止，最晚於 2027 年 6 月
30 日公布最終決定。MSCI 原先於今年 1 月提出可能降至「邊境市場（Frontier Market
）」，此次則跳過「邊境市場」提出直接將該國股市降至「獨立市場」的方案，顯示對

印尼市場治理的疑慮進一步加深。雖然「邊境市場」仍可能是替代分類方案，但 MSCI
強調，除非有明確證據顯示市場治理及運作出現實質改善，否則預期將依目前提案重
新分類，顯示此次降評風險較過去顯著升高。
2. 股權透明度與交易秩序問題為 MSCI 主要疑慮：MSCI 指出，印尼股市長期存在股權
結構不透明、實際自由流通股比例難以確認、疑似協調交易，以及市場監理執法成效
不足等問題，影響國際投資人對市場公平性與可投資性的信心。雖然印尼主管機關已
提出強化持股超過 1%股東資訊揭露、建立高持股集中度（HSC）監理機制，以及規
劃逐步將最低自由流通股比例提高至 15%等改革措施，但 MSCI 認為相關改革尚未展
現持續且具實質性的改善成果。由於問題涉及市場制度與治理結構，改善所需時間可
能較一般短期市場波動更長。
3. 股市反彈後再度回落，外資撤離壓力仍高：受降評消息衝擊，印尼雅加達綜合指數 10
月 16 日重挫 11.57%，創 1998 年以來最大單日跌幅，19 日再跌 3.64%，盤中最低

觸及 4,907.37 點。雖然 20 日至 23 日連續四個交易日反彈，但 26 日再度下跌 1.22%
，收於 5,373.09 點，各產業類股全面收黑，顯示市場信心仍不穩定。根據 Reuters
報導，截至 10 月 19 日，2026 年外資已累計淨賣超印尼股票約 48.8 億美元。此外，
印尼證交所 26 日宣布，將引進獨立專家及外國投資人代表參與高持股集中度審查，
並自 11 月起加強調查疑似協調交易，必要時凍結可疑帳戶，以回應 MSCI 對市場制
度的疑慮。
二、 下跌原因分析：
1. 股市：潛在指數剔除與資金外流，持續壓抑市場評價
若印尼股市最終降至「獨立市場」，原則上將不再納入 MSCI 新興市場及邊境市場相關
指數，追蹤相關指數的被動式基金將須依規則調整持股，實際處理方式仍以 MSCI 後
續公告為準。若改降至「邊境市場」，則可能獲得部分邊境市場資金承接，衝擊相對較
小。Reuters 引述多家金融機構估計，印尼股市降至獨立市場可能引發約 30 億至 60

美元潛在撤資風險，惟實際規模仍視最終分類結果及基金策略而定。
2. 匯率：外資撤離增加印尼盾貶值壓力
印尼股市的降評風險不僅衝擊股市，也可能透過外資撤離增加印尼盾貶值壓力。印尼
盾 10 月 16 日盤中一度貶至 18,070 兌 1 美元，為 7 月下旬以來最弱水準之一，當日
收盤約 18,000。若後續國際資金持續流出，可能進一步增加股匯市波動，對以外幣計
價、且未完全避險的印尼基金及 ETF 形成額外壓力。雖然印尼央行可透過匯市干預及

流動性管理措施穩定金融市場，但在資金外流壓力尚未緩解前，印尼盾仍可能維持偏
弱格局。
3. 基金與 ETF：指數權重下滑，單一市場曝險風險升高
MSCI 相關指數調整已對印尼股票產生實質影響。Reuters 引用 LSEG 資料顯示，印尼
在 MSCI 新興市場指數的權重已由 2026 年初的 1.16%降至 9 月底的 0.43%，相關成
分股亦由年初 18 檔減至 9 檔，反映部分指數資金調整壓力已提前出現。對印尼單一
國家基金及 ETF 而言，主要風險來自股市下跌、資金流出及印尼盾波動；主動式基金
雖可透過選股降低部分影響，但仍難以完全迴避整體市場風險。不同產品實際受到的
影響，仍應依追蹤指數、主要持股及匯率避險安排個別評估。
三、 市場看法：
1. 制度性問題難以速解，降評風險將持續牽動市場：我們認為，此次 MSCI 降評風險並
非單純反映經濟成長放緩或企業獲利不佳，而是涉及市場透明度、交易公平性與監理
執法等結構性問題。尤其 MSCI 不僅啟動降評諮詢，更持續限制印尼股票的部分指數

調整，包括不提高外資納入因子、不新增相關成分股，以及不進行向上規模分類調整
，顯示市場可投資性疑慮尚未獲得有效解決。儘管政府試圖推動改革，但制度修正仍
須經過執行與成效驗證，才能重新取得國際投資人的信任，因此短期內降評風險仍難
完全消除。
2. 外資撤離與指數權重下降，市場信心修復恐需較長時間：印尼今年以來外資已大幅賣
超，MSCI 新興市場指數中的權重亦明顯下降，顯示國際投資人對當地市場的疑慮已
逐漸反映於實際資金流向。我們認為，即使印尼最終成功避免降評，部分國際機構投
資人仍可能因市場透明度、流動性及治理品質疑慮而維持較低曝險。此外，MSCI 對
部分指數調整的凍結措施尚未解除，短期內新增指數資金流入機會仍受到限制，預期
國際資金回流與市場評價修復均可能需要較長時間。
3. 監理改革力度加大，但尚不足以扭轉保守展望：印尼政府近期持續加強改革，包括提
高資訊透明度、強化交易監控、對違規交易開罰，以及引進外部獨立人士參與監理審
查。10 月 26 日該國政府進一步提出主動調查疑似協調交易及凍結可疑帳戶等措施，

11 月 2 日起亦規劃將高持股集中度股票排除於雅加達綜合指數之外。相關改革有助改

確性不做任何擔保，如有錯漏或疏忽，本行及本行之任何董事或受僱人，毋須負任何法律責任。任何人因信賴此等資料而做出或改變投資決
策，應審慎衡量本身之需求及投資風險，自行承擔一切投資風險並自負盈虧，本行不負任何法律責任。本報告受中華民國著作權法保護為元
大銀行所有，非經本行同意，不得將本報告內容之一部或全部加以複製、轉載或散布。本報告所載述的意見本行可隨時予以更改或撤回，恕
不另行通知。
善交易秩序及短期市場信心，但部分措施仍待正式實施，且實際成效尚未獲得 MSCI
認可。考量制度性問題改善需要時間，政策利多雖可能帶動短線反彈，但尚不足以改
變對印尼股市中期前景的保守判斷。
4. 股市跌深不代表利空出盡，中期風險報酬吸引力偏低：印尼股市在 10 月 16 日及 19
日大幅修正後，雖曾連續四個交易日反彈，但 26 日再度回落，顯示市場尚未形成穩
定回升趨勢。我們認為，當前股市面臨的不僅是短期賣壓，更包括國際投資人信任下
降及風險溢價提高，即使企業基本面未同步惡化，仍可能因評價下修而承受股價壓力

。因此，短線不排除政策利多或跌深後出現技術性反彈，但在 MSCI 態度、外資流向
與印尼盾走勢明顯改善前，反彈延續性仍有疑慮，印尼股市中期展望維持保守。
四、市場展望：
1. 印尼股市維持保守展望，單一市場風險報酬吸引力不足：考量 MSCI 降評風險尚未解除、
外資撤離壓力持續，以及市場治理改革仍有待驗證，我們認為印尼股市未來數季的風險報
酬吸引力相對有限。即使近期股價明顯回落，仍難認定相關風險已充分反映。相較市場制
度成熟、國際資金流動性較佳的新興市場，印尼股市可能持續面臨較高風險溢價，因此對
單一印尼市場的集中曝險應維持審慎態度。
2. 此事件對新興市場指數與東協指數影響相對有限：根據 MSCI 資料，截至 2026 年 9 月底
，印尼股市在 MSCI Emerging Market 指數與 MSCI AC ASEAN 指數中的權重分別約為
0.43%與 7.55%，因此我們認為印尼潛在降評對相關指數的直接衝擊相對有限。此外，新
興市場各國經濟結構與產業組成有所差異，區域型基金可透過跨國分散降低單一市場風險

。惟後續仍須留意印尼事件對整體新興市場投資信心造成的外溢影響，以及全球貿易與匯
率波動風險。

## Document 146: 261111_daiwa_wiwynn

Source PDF: all report pdf/261111_daiwa_wiwynn.pdf
Report source: line報告備份
Date: 2026-11-11
Pages: 7
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Taiwan Information Technology

What’s new: We update our view after 3Q26 results (issued 6 November).

What’s the impact: Fundamentals largely on track. Rising CPU demand 
from agentic AI has lifted our 2026 regular server growth target to >50% 
YoY (vs. 20-30% YoY), making regular servers the key growth driver for 
Wiwynn and helping it offset the slower-than-expected AMZN ASIC AI 
server ramp in 3Q26 (ramping from September vs. original July estimate). 
Looking ahead, we expect the T3 lifecycle to likely peak in 2Q/3Q27, but 
the rising contribution from both Nvidia and AMD GPU AI servers should 
offset the slowdown in 2H27. As for the margin trend, we expect regular 
servers to be at 10% and ASIC AI servers to be at 8%. However, we expect 
the rising GPU AI server to gradually dilute the blended gross margin as 
Wiwynn focuses only on L11, while GPU motherboard (L6) and tray level 
(L10) will be handled by its parent company Wistron (3231 TT, TWD171, 
Buy [1]). As such, we expect the gross margin for Wiwynn’s GPU AI servers 
to be 2-3%, lower than the 4-5% AI of other ODM peers.

3Q26 results beat expectations. Wiwynn reported 3Q26 net income of 
TWD23.36bn (EPS: TWD41.46), beating our expectation by 8%. 3Q26 
sales (TWD434.62bn; up 56% QoQ and 63% YoY) beat our estimate by 6% 
and the Bloomberg consensus estimate by 7% on stronger-than-expected 
regular server shipments. Regular servers accounted for 50-55% of its 
revenue, down from 60-70% in 2Q26, while AI servers contributed 45-50%. 
Gross margin declined by 0.4pp QoQ to 8.9%, but still exceeded our 8.2% 
estimate. Management attributed the sequential margin contraction mainly 
to lower NRE recognition and rising material costs, as not all memory 
components used in regular servers are procured under consignment 
arrangements. However, we believe lower regular server contribution to the 
product mix was key reason. With better operating leverage, operating 
margin improved by 0.2pp QoQ to 7.5%. That said, net profit was dragged 
down by higher interest expense (reaching TWD3bn in 3Q26 vs. TWD2bn 
in 2Q26) and a higher tax rate of 23.2% (vs. 21.1% in 2Q26).

What we recommend: We lift our 2026-27E EPS by 1-5% to reflect 
stronger regular server demand but cut 2028E EPS by 3% to reflect higher 
interest expense assumption. We reaffirm our Buy (1) rating but trim our 
12M TP to TWD2,500 (from TWD2,715), based on a target PER of 13x 
(prior: 18x; within its past-2-year trading range of 9-21x) applied to our 1-
year forward diluted EPS, given weaker market sentiment. Key downside 
risks: weaker-than-expected server demand and CSP capex.

How we differ: Our 2028E EPS is 3% above street estimates due to our 
higher regular and AI server shipment assumptions.

11 November 2026

Company profile 
Established in 2012 and listed in 2019, Wiwynn (a subsidiary of Wistron Corp) is a dedicated ODM-
direct supplier of servers for hyperscale datacentres, with major customers including Facebook, 
Microsoft, and Amazon. Aside from hyperscaler customers, the company is expanding its target markets 
to what it terms "Next Wave" customers, including tier-2 cloud service providers and telecom operators, 
which could bring growth opportunities related to edge computing and open RAN.

### Page 5

5

Wiwynn Corp (6669 TT): 11 November 2026 
ESG analysis

Update Date: 11 Nov 2026 
Source: Daiwa, Company

ESG risks
Risks Management Analyst comments
G
Executive/board
quality 2
Wiwynn plans to have seven to nine directors on its board who will serve three-year terms in
accordance with the Articles of Incorporation in Taiwan. Currently, Wiwynn has 9 board
members with five independent directors. The background of independent directors is diversified
and their competence includes technology, business management, and law. We deem the
diversified background as a positive factor for the company’s board quality. Wiwynn held 9 board
meetings in 2025 and the average attendance rate was around 99%.
The Chairman does not serve concurrently as the President or CEO, which we think is a positive
for board and executive management as well as external shareholder interest.
Capital management 2
Wiwynn declared cash DPS of TWD145 and a stock dividend of TWD20 per share for 2025,
representing a total payout ratio of 60.0%. We note that investors favour its high dividend payout
ratio policy. However, Wiwynn has been expanding its own manufacturing capacity in Malaysia,
Tainan, Mexico and the US, making its business model less asset-light than before. With the
server market still in the high growth period and the payout ratio remaining at around 60%, we
expect the favourable dividend policy to be sustained going forward.
Related party &
transaction 3
In 2025, revenue from related parties accounted for 0.2% of total revenue, while purchases from
related parties were 9.2% of cost of sales. The main purchase counterparty is Wistron (3231 TT;
Wiwynn's parent company), with purchases from Wistron accounting for 8.09% of total
procurement. While Wiwynn is a professional assembly vendor for data center servers, it still
outsources a portion of its production capacity to its parent company, Wistron. At the same time,
Wiwynn has expanded its own production bases in Malaysia, Tainan, Mexico and the US,
reducing its reliance on Wistron. This business model still causes meaningful transactions with
related parties, although the exposure has declined materially. Indeed, we are concerned
whether Wistron collects fair manufacturing fees from Wiwynn. As the company states that
trading terms of purchase transactions with related parties cannot be compared with third-party
vendors due to product specifications, we are not able to assess this concern.
S Supply chain management 1
Wiwynn focuses on providing various products and system solutions for hyperscale datacentres
and cloud infrastructures. There are over 100 suppliers providing key components, electronic
materials, and mechanical materials in Wiwynn’s supply chain. For the new joiners that target to
penetrate into Wiwynn’s supply chain, Wiwynn has formed the “Supplier Evaluation Group” to
conduct: 1) supplier management and supply capabilities evaluation, 2) technical capability
development assessment, and 3) partner quality assurance (these include mainly topics
undertaken by RBA, such as no child labour, no conflict minerals, no use of environmentally
hazardous substances, etc.). New suppliers are also required to be ISO9001, ISO14001, IECQ
QC 080000, and OHSAS 18001 (ISO 45001) qualified.
Wiwynn has strong supply chain risk management by conducting audits regularly. Apart from
considering the technical skills, quality date, delivery dates and price completeness of suppliers,
Wiwynn audits its suppliers to endeavour to protect the environment, ensure safety and health of
the environment, and emphasise the importance of human rights, based on Wiwynn’s CSR
Management System.
S Data security 2
To serve world-class cloud service providers, Wiwynn has been constantly improving its
information security management. Employees are educated on the protection of the product,
technology, and information privacy of customers.
In response to the global trends in data protection laws that tend to strengthen the security of
classified information, Wiwynn has mandated that all assigned PCs have the Windows Bitlocker
HDD encryption activated to avoid classified data being accessed without permission. In 2020, it
also introduced Multi-Factor Authentication (MFA). Beyond the security measures for hardware,
the company controls access to office, labs, and conference room in a robust manner to prevent
theft of the hardware.
Note: Management score represents a company's ability to manage/benefit from certain ESG topics. The scores range from 1 to 3, with 1 being the strongest.

## Document 174: 261113_daiwa_Gigabyte

### Page 1

Summary: Gigabyte released its 3Q26 results after market hours on 13 November, with 
net income of TWD8.57bn (EPS: TWD12.07) beating expectations, while revenue of 
TWD154.7bn (+7% QoQ, +94% YoY) was largely in line despite robust server demand. 
Gross margin expanded 4.4pp QoQ to 14.0% (vs. our 9.6% estimate). Management cited 
factors such as higher value-added products and better cost control, though we believe 
VGA card price hikes were the key driver. Despite strong gross margin expansion and 
operating leverage, higher profit-sharing bonuses limited OPM expansion to 2.7pp QoQ to 
8.3%, still above our/consensus estimates of 5.8%/5.9%. We expect Gigabyte to benefit 
from solid AI server demand from neoclouds as they increase power budgets and capex, 
with neoclouds and enterprises becoming key Nvidia server buyers from Vera Rubin in 
2027. Nebius reaffirmed its 5GW contracted-power and 0.8-1GW connected-power end-
2026 targets, with >1GW annual deployments from 2027; CoreWeave raised its 2026 
capex guidance to USD37-39bn, with USD132.5bn in backlog. As neoclouds serve 
sovereign and enterprise customers lacking in-house hardware capabilities, we expect 
TAM growth for server brands including Dell, Gigabyte, Lenovo and Asustek. We reaffirm 
our AI server revenue forecasts of TWD412bn in 2026E and TWD565bn in 2027E. We 
expect the AI server mix to dilute the GM to 8.9% in 2027E and 8.4% in 2028E. We 
reiterate our Buy (1) rating with a 12M TP of TWD490, based on 15x the 1-year-forward 
diluted EPS; key downside risk is Nvidia’s business model change.

What’s the impact: 
Below are our read-throughs from Gigabyte’s 3Q26 results (released on 13 November).

• 3Q26 results first-take: profitability beat expectations. Gigabyte’s net income of 
TWD8.57bn (EPS: TWD12.07) beat our/Bloomberg consensus estimates by 32%/29%, 
respectively. Revenue (TWD154.7bn; up 7% QoQ and 94% YoY) was largely in-line with 
estimates, despite robust server demand. Nevertheless, the gross margin expanded by 
4.4pp QoQ to 14.0% in 3Q26 (exceeding our estimate of 9.6%). Management attributed 
the strength to an improved product mix, higher value-added products, strategic 
procurement, and better cost control, but we believe price hikes in VGA cards to be the 
key factor. Despite strong gross margin expansion and operating leverage, higher profit-
sharing bonus expenses caused the operating margin to only rise by 2.7pp QoQ to 8.3% 
(still beating our/street’s estimates of 5.8%/5.9%).

• Business outlook. We believe Gigabyte will ride on consistently solid AI server demand 
from the neoclouds, as key vendors continue to raise their power budget and capex. 
This aligns with our view that the neoclouds and enterprise will gradually become key

buyers of Nvidia AI servers starting from the Vera Rubin generation in 2027. Nebius 
(NBIS US, NR) reaffirmed its end-2026 contracted-power target of 5GW (up from >4GW 
currently contracted) and connected-power target of 0.8-1GW, with plans to deploy 
>1GW annually starting in 2027. Meanwhile, Coreweave (CRWV US, NR) raised its 
2026 capex guidance to USD37-39bn (from USD35-39bn) with an order backlog of 
USD132.5bn (vs. USD3.7bn revenue in 3Q26). We believe the neoclouds will become a 
key computing source for sovereign, enterprise and other corporate customers that 
cannot design and maintain the hardware themselves. As such, we expect the TAM for 
server brands like Dell (DELL US, USD371.17, Outperform [2], covered by Louis 
Miscioscia), Gigabyte, Lenovo (992 HK, NR) and Asustek (2357 TT, NR) to rise.

While management expects continued revenue growth and improving operating 
leverage in 2027, we reaffirm our AI server revenue assumptions for Gigabyte of 
TWD412bn in 2026E and TWD565bn in 2027E (vs. TWD180bn in 2025). We also 
believe the rising contribution from AI servers will be structurally margin-dilutive, despite 
potential benefits from higher value-added solutions, strategic procurement, and 
economies of scale. We therefore forecast gross margin to gradually normalise to 8.9% 
in 2027E and 8.4% in 2028E (vs. 14.0% in 3Q26).

What we recommend: 
We have a Buy (1) rating on Gigabyte, with a 12M TP of TWD490, based on a target PER 
of 15x, applied to our 1-year-forward diluted EPS. Key downside risk: earnings impact from 
Nvidia’s business model change.

Greater China Technology Hardware
Asia Pacific
Industry View In-Line
4Q NB build forecast cut 5% despite October’s beat: October ODM NB shipments
came in 4% above MSe, but we lower our 4Q26 build forecast by 5% to 21.9M units
(-13% q/q, -32% y/y) to reflect the latest ODM guidance. The revised forecast implies

limited quarter-end pull-in strength and a deeper y/y contraction than the 26%
decline in 3Q26. Despite an already depressed 3Q base, the projected 13%
sequential decline is well below the 15-year average of flat q/q shipments in 4Q.
PC outlook seen continuing to weaken into 2027: NB build trends have weakened
over recent months, while increasingly cautious ODM commentary provides further
evidence of pressure on PC demand. Asustek expects global PC unit shipments to

decline 10–15% y/y in 2027 (link), while Quanta expects global NB shipments to
drop by high single digits to 10% y/y (link). We believe 2027 outlooks from the
supply chain have worsened in recent months, and both outlooks are directionally
consistent with our forecast for an 11.5% y/y decline in global PC units in 2027.
Looking to 1Q27, our preliminary view is a further sequential decline in NB builds,
although we have yet to establish a formal forecast.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -
relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.

주가수익률 (%) 1개월 3개월 12개월  
 절대 (16.4) (17.3) 13.4   
 상대 (3.3) (21.1) (22.1)  
 절대 (달러환산) (15.2) (10.5) 21.4

3Q26 OP 2.7조, 당사 추정치 35% 상회 
서프라이즈의 근거가 판매보증비 감소에서 발생. 3Q26 기말환율의 낙폭 (-12% qoq)으로 
인한 역설적 비용절감의 규모가 전년동기 대비 1.2조 원을 넘어서며 환율의 조사모삼 효과
로 만들어진 컨센서스 상회 실적. 
긍정적인 부분: 컨틴전시, 신차 효과, 인센티브, 생산 정상화 
지속적인 컨틴전시 체제를 유지하며 비용절감 실천 (=지속된 요소). 4분기 추가적으로 나아
질 요인은 1) 내수 신차효과로 판가 상승 및 유기적 수익성 개선, 2) 공장 가동률 정상화를 
통한 고정비 부담 절감, 3) 해외시장 신차효과를 통한 딜러 인센티브 절감. 
경계할 부분: 재료비, 환율, BEV 판매비중 확대 
재료비 측면에서 1) 2Q26 시작된 미국-이란 전쟁 관련 원재료비 상승은 4Q26F에도 하락
세를 보이지 못할 것이며, 2) 구리, 철강 등의 금속 원자재의 가격 상승 또한 4Q26F 지속될 
것. 또한 3Q26과 반대로 qoq 기말환율 대비 낙폭 확대할 평균환율 (mtd 1,338원) 효과로 
환 관련 감익 본격화, IONIQ 3의 출시는 인센티브 절감인 동시에 믹스 관점에서 부정적. 
투자의견 BUY 유지, 목표주가 440,000원으로 소폭 상향 
목표주가 상향 근거는 컨틴전시 체제 유지를 통한 FY27F 자동차사업 부문 이익 예상치 상
향. 부정적 외생변수 (환율, 재료비)가 지속된다는 전제 하에 FY27F OPM 하락을 예상하나, 
(FY26F 5.6%, FY27F 5.2%) 향후 신차효과와 생산 정상화에 따른 자동차 사업부문 수익성 
반등의 여력 존재. 주가 상승 트리거는 1) CES 전후 BD 관련 기대감, 2) 신차 출시 이후 견
조한 판매량, 3) 환율, 원재료비 등 외생변수의 개선.

투자의견 BUY  
매수, 유지 
6개월 
목표주가 540,000 
하향  
현재주가 
(26.10.28) 
285,500 
조선업종

◼ 글로벌 피어 평균 멀티플 하락을 반영하여 목표주가 하향 
◼ 3Q에 산재한 일회성 요인. 원화강세, 조업일수 감소, 임단협 비용 반영 
◼ 모두의 우려에도 불구하고 본업은 안정적으로 천천히 잘 진행되는 중

투자의견 매수, 목표주가 540,000원으로 -32.5% 하향 
목표주가는 28F EPS 42,343원에 글로벌 조선사 피어 평균 12.8배를 적용해 
산출함. 목표주가 하향은 글로벌 경기 불확실성과 중국 조선소 생산캐파 확장
으로 인한 공급과잉 리스크로 전반적인 산업 멀티플이 하락한 것을 반영함. 
다만 가스선 수주 모멘텀과 발전용 엔진의 수주 및 생산 확대 등에 따른 중장
기 성장 유효

3분기는 원래 비수기입니다 
동사의 2026년 3분기 매출액은 5조 7,536억원(+30.2% YoY, -9.1% QoQ), 영
업이익은 8,992억원(+61.3% YoY, -13.5% QoQ), OPM 15.6%를 기록. 3분기는 
1)원달러 환율 하락(전분기 대비 평균 -79원) 2)연휴로 인한 조업일수 감소(47
일), 3)임단협 관련 추가 발생 가능 비용 75% 반영)의 영향이 가장 컸음

중장기적인 모멘텀은 여전히 살아있다 
[상선] 중국 공급 확대에 따른 선가 및 수주 경쟁 심화는 부담이나 LNG선 발
주 재개 및 노후선 교체 수요는 긍정적. 동사의 고부가가치선 위주의 수주 전
략 방향은 유지될 예정 [해양] 루야 프로젝트 진행률 인식으로 4분기도 양호
한 이익률 기대, 다만 현재 수주 부진으로 블록, 혹은 LNG선 투입을 통해 매
출 하락을 방어하는 방안도 구상 중 [방산] 키윗과의 미국 합작 조선소를 통해 
콘솔탱커, 로로선 등 지원함 건조를 시작으로 전투함까지 확장 준비 [엔진] 국
가전력망 및 데이터센터향 발전 수요 확대, 하이퍼스케일러 직접협의로 성장
성 강화, 수주 확정 및 추진 물량 기준 28년 1.5GW, 29년 3GW 생산 계획 중

## Document 268: [373220] LG에너지솔루션(373220) 보상금 제외해도 호실적LG에너지솔루션(373220) 보상금 제외해도 호실적보상금 제외해도 호실적 - 기업

Source PDF: all report pdf/[373220] LG에너지솔루션(373220) 보상금 제외해도 호실적LG에너지솔루션(373220) 보상금 제외해도 호실적보상금 제외해도 호실적 - 기업.pdf
Report source: Hankyung
Date: 2026-10-12
Pages: 10
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

(373220) 
LG 에너지솔루션 
2026.10.12

에너지/인프라/배터리 황성현, Ph.D. 
6878 / tjdgus2009@eugenefn.com

보상금 제외해도 호실적 
 투자의견 BUY(유지) 
 목표주가 530,000 원(상향) 
 현재주가 401,000 원(10/8)

 [투자의견] 당사는 LG에너지솔루션에 대해 실적 추정치 상향을 반영하여 목
표주가를 530,000원으로 13% 상향하고, 투자의견 BUY를 유지한다. 
3Q26부터 ESS 물량 증가에 따른 가동률 상승 효과가 본격적으로 실적에 
반영되기 시작했으며, 4Q26에도 AMPC 제외 흑자 기조를 이어갈 전망이
다. 전사 가동률은 3분기 65%까지 상승한 것으로 파악되며, 이에 따른 펀
더멘털 개선이 본격화되고 있다고 판단한다. 
 [3Q26 잠정 실적 Review] 매출액 9.6조원(+28%qoq, +59%yoy), 영업이
익 7,560억원(+567%qoq, +26%yoy)을 기록하며 당사 추정을 큰 폭으로 
상회했다. 북미 생산보조금(AMPC)을 제외한 매출액은 9.2조원, 영업이익은 
3,391억원(영업이익률 3.7%)으로, 2분기(-1,277억원, -1.7%) 대비 흑자 
전환에 성공했다. ① 원통형은 테슬라향 물량 호조에 힘입어 영업이익 
2,010억원(영업이익률 8.1%)을 달성한 것으로 추정된다. ② EV는 유럽 폴
란드 공장의 가동률 상승과 GM 얼티엄의 8월 가동 개시로 매출이 증가했
다. 여기에 완성차 업체 보상금 3,000억원이 반영되며 영업이익 1,090억
원(영업이익률 2.5%)을 기록한 것으로 추정된다. ③ ESS는 매출액이 50% 
이상 급증하며 실적 서프라이즈를 견인했다. AMPC 포함 영업이익은 4,460
억원(영업이익률 16.0%)으로 추정된다. 2분기 매출 성장률 +17%qoq에 
이어 3분기 성장세가 더욱 가팔라졌다는 점이 긍정적이다. 
 [4Q26 Preview] 매출액 8.0조원(-17%qoq, +30%yoy), 영업이익 6,790
억원(-10%qoq, 흑전yoy)으로 추정한다. 4분기 실적은 ESS 수익성 개선 
속도와 완성차 업체 보상금 반영 규모에 따라 변동할 전망이다. 동사는 기존 
가이던스를 통해 하반기 북미 ESS 생산량이 상반기 대비 2배 증가할 것으
로 제시한 바 있다. 3분기 ESS는 AMPC를 제외하더라도 양호한 수익성을 
기록한 것으로 추정된다. 향후 팩 생산 병목 해소와 북미 공장 가동 안정화
에 따른 고정비 부담 완화가 이어지며 추가적인 손익 개선이 가능할 전망이
다. EV 역시 GM 얼티엄 생산 확대와 유럽 공장 가동률 상승이 지속되며 
AMPC 제외 적자 규모를 점진적으로 축소할 것으로 예상한다. 
 [투자포인트] 9월 미시간 랜싱 공장의 개소식이 개최됐다. 랜싱 공장은 당초 
EV용 파우치 배터리 생산거점으로 계획됐으나 ESS 중심으로 역할이 재편
됐으며, 내년에는 10GWh 이상의 각형 LFP 생산라인이 추가될 예정이다. 
2027년에는 신규 대규모 증설보다 기존 북미 생산거점의 가동률 상승과 
ESS 생산량 확대를 통한 수익성 개선이 핵심이 될 전망이다. 북미 전력망 
접속 지연과 AI 데이터센터의 부하 변동성 확대에 따라 Stand-alone ESS, 
BTM, UPS, BBU 수요가 증가하면서 신규 수주도 지속 확대될 것으로 예상
한다. 특히 동사의 Non-PFE 요건 충족과 SI· O&M을 아우르는 End-to-
End 솔루션 역량이 북미 ESS 시장에서 경쟁력으로 작용할 전망이다.

당사는 자료 작성일 기준으로 지난 3 개월 간 해당종목에 대해서 유가증권 발행에 참여한 적이 없습니다 
당사는 본 자료 발간일을 기준으로 해당종목의 주식을 1% 이상 보유하고 있지 않습니다  
당사는 동 자료를 기관투자가 또는 제 3 자에게 사전 제공한 사실이 없습니다 
조사분석담당자는 자료작성일 현재 동 종목과 관련하여 재산적 이해관계가 없습니다 
동 자료에 게재된 내용들은 조사분석담당자 본인의 의견을 정확하게 반영하고 있으며, 외부의 부당한 압력이나 간섭 없이 작성되었음을 확인합니다 
동 자료는 당사의 제작물로서 모든 저작권은 당사에게 있습니다 
동 자료는 당사의 동의 없이 어떠한 경우에도 어떠한 형태로든 복제, 배포, 전송, 변형, 대여할 수 없습니다 
동 자료에 수록된 내용은 당사 리서치센터가 신뢰할 만한 자료 및 정보로부터 얻어진 것이나, 당사는 그 정확성이나 완전성을 보장할 수 없습니다. 따라서 어떠
한 경우에 동 자료는 고객의 주식투자의 결과에 대한 법적 책임소재에 대한 증빙자료로 사용될 수 없습니다

## Document 277: AI繁荣终结？美银月度调查：基金经理20年来最大幅度削减科技股仓位

Source PDF: all report pdf/AI繁荣终结？美银月度调查：基金经理20年来最大幅度削减科技股仓位.pdf
Report source: Unknown
Date: 未知日期
Pages: 3
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

美银调查显⽰，全球基⾦经理科技股超配⽐例从 10 ⽉的 37% 骤降⾄ 12% ，单⽉降幅创近 20
年之最，创纪录的 38% 受访者直指 AI 存在过度投资。美银策略师直⾔ "AI 繁荣与亢奋情绪已
经终结 " 。与此同时，净 67% 的基⾦经理预计欧股将在未来⼀⾄三个⽉内上涨，为今年 2 ⽉
以来最⾼读数。
摘要：
美国银⾏（ Bank of America ）最新⽉度调查显⽰，全球基⾦经理正在以 2006 年以来最快速度撤
出科技股，对⼈⼯智能过度投资的担忧达到历史峰值。同时，基⾦经理对欧洲股市的乐观程度
升⾄年内最⾼，呈现出鲜明的跨市场分化格局。
调查显⽰，科技股超配⽐例从 10 ⽉的净 37% 骤降⾄净 12% ，单⽉降幅创近 20 年之最。与此同
时，欧洲股市⽅⾯，净 67% 的基⾦经理预计欧洲股市将在未来⼀⾄三个⽉内上涨，为今年 2 ⽉以
来最⾼读数。
美银策略师 Michael Hartnett 在报告中直⾔ "AI 繁荣与亢奋情绪已经终结 " ，并建议投资者在未来
数⽉维持防御性仓位。

市场表现印证了这⼀情绪转变。纳斯达克 100 指数本⽉已下跌逾 4% ，料创 2008 年以来最差 11 ⽉
表现；半导体相关股票指数⾃ 10 ⽉初以来累计下跌 27% 。
这⼀调查于 11 ⽉ 6 ⽇⾄ 12 ⽇进⾏，全球部分共覆盖 169 名参与者，管理资产规模合计 4610 亿美
元；欧洲区域性问题由 94 名参与者作答，管理资产规模合计 2410 亿美元。
科技股遭⼤规模减仓， AI 过投忧虑创纪录
基⾦经理对科技股的热情正在快速消退。调查显⽰，科技股超配⽐例从 10 ⽉的净 37% 降⾄净
12% ，降幅为 2006 年 6 ⽉以来最⼤。
对⼈⼯智能投资回报的质疑是这⼀转变的核⼼驱动⼒。创纪录的净 38% 受访者认为企业在 AI 领
域存在过度投资， 52% 的受访者预计未来六个⽉内将有 AI 超⼤规模计算运营商宣布削减资本⽀
出。
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⻅闻历 搜索

### Page 2

⻛险提⽰及免责条款
市场有⻛险，投资需谨慎。本⽂不构成个⼈投资建议，也未考虑到个别⽤⼾特殊的投资⽬标、财务状况或需要。⽤⼾应考虑本⽂
中的任何意⻅、观点或结论是否符合其特定状况。据此投资，责任⾃负。
40 收藏 分享到 :
Hartnett 将此形容为投资者向企业发出的 " 重要 " 信号，要求暂停 AI ⽀出。
Hartnett 建议，从历史规律来看，最佳⼊场时机出现在基⾦经理将科技股仓位进⼀步削减⾄低配
之后，⽬前调查尚未显⽰出⾜够的投资者 " 恐慌 " 情绪，不宜仓促⼊场。
衰退担忧有限，多家机构仍看好后市
尽管科技股承压，基⾦经理对宏观经济前景的担忧相对温和。仅约 3% 的受访者预计未来⼀年内
出现衰退，现⾦持仓占总资产⽐例降⾄ 3.6% ，为 8 ⽉以来最低⽔平。
其他机构对股市整体仍持乐观⽴场。摩根⼠丹利本周表⽰， AI ⽀出热潮将使 2027 年成为 " 拥抱⻛
险之年 " ；⾼盛预计标普 500 指数未来 12 个⽉将上涨 17% ，⽬标点位 8700 点。
在资产配置上，约 36% 的基⾦经理预计国际股票将成为 2027 年表现最佳的资产类别，其次是美

国股票（ 24% ）和⻩⾦（ 17% ）。
欧洲股市乐观情绪升温，盈利预期改善
相较于全球科技股的降温，欧洲股市的投资情绪明显向好。
净 67% 的基⾦经理预计欧洲股市未来⼀⾄三个⽉内上涨，⾼于上⽉的净 44% ，为 2 ⽉以来最⾼⽔
平。受访者预计欧洲股市未来 12 个⽉平均涨幅为 5.92% ，⾼于此前的 4.53% ；每股收益平均增⻓
预期亦从 6.09% 上调⾄ 6.58% 。
从盈利驱动逻辑来看，成本管控已成为 43% 基⾦经理眼中欧洲企业盈利的⾸要驱动因素，为 5 ⽉
以来⾸次登顶； 37% 的受访者认为销量或营收增⻓是主要驱动⼒， 13% 指向利润率扩张。
宏观层⾯，净 40% 的受访者预计欧洲经济增⻓将在未来 12 个⽉内改善，明显⾼于此前的 22% 。
⾏业层⾯，⼯业品与服务板块⾃ 2025 年 9 ⽉以来⾸次成为超配⽐例最⾼的板块，建筑和医疗保健
板块也从低配转为超配，基础资源和能源板块则回归中性。
汽⻋板块依然是最受冷落的板块，净低配⽐例⾼达 47% 。在未来 12 个⽉最看好的板块中，约

23% 受访者⾸选医疗保健，⾮必需消费品和⾦融板块紧随其后，各占 17% 。
在各国市场中，瑞⼠以净偏好⽐例 30% 成为最受⻘睐的市场，德国以 27% 位居其次。法国则以
净偏好⽐例 -60% 垫底，创历史新低。

## Document 317: Cliff Notes_ cost of living front and centre (2026-11-17)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Cliff Notes_ cost of living front and centre (2026-11-17).pdf
Report source: Unknown
Date: 未知日期
Pages: 11
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

#cliffnotes #rba #australia #inflation #us #fomc #ecb #aud #usd
Cliff Notes: cost of living front and centre
By Ryan Wells, Elliot Clarke & Illiana Jain
09:49 November 13 2026 Share
Key insights from the week that was.
In Australia, the Westpac-MI Consumer Sentiment Index fell 2.7% to 78.2 in November, its weakest
reading since late-2022 and a level historically associated with periods of severe economic stress.
Renewed cost-of-living and interest rate pressures were front and centre following another lift in

inflation, including sharply higher petrol prices, and the RBA’s November rate increase. Mortgage
holders are feeling the squeeze most acutely, with sentiment across this group falling to a record low.

### Page 2

Consumers have become noticeably more downbeat on the near-term economic outlook – the relevant
sub-index dropping 10.2% to be 22% below its long-run average. Job-loss fears also continue to edge
up.
Businesses are similarly troubled. The latest NAB business survey showed confidence slipped further
to –15 in October as conditions held near their weakest level since the pandemic. Of concern for the
outlook, forward orders fell to –12, also the weakest read since the pandemic, and capital expenditure

intentions deteriorated. Purchase costs continue to grow materially faster than selling prices, further
compressing already tight margins.
Housing provides a clear example of how caution and tighter financial conditions can jolt activity. The
value of new dwelling finance approvals plunged 15% in Q3, the largest quarterly decline in the series’
history, taking approvals 25% lower year-to-date. Investor lending bore the brunt of the adjustment,
falling 30% over the quarter as higher rates combined with the housing tax changes announced in May.

Owner-occupier lending also weakened, however, with first-home buyers proving particularly sensitive
to the deterioration in affordability and market conditions.
Taken together, the data points to a further loss of momentum across the domestic economy,
consistent with Westpac’s expectation for subdued growth and concern over downside activity risks.
Meanwhile, in the US, October’s headline CPI met our expectations but at 0.4% was a touch above the

consensus view. Energy and food both contributed meaningfully to the result, but core inflation was
also marginally stronger than forecast, +0.3%. Core goods inflation has accelerated over the past four
months to average a gain of 0.2% as businesses look to pass tariffs, tech and energy input cost
inflation through. Shelter meanwhile continued to rise at an above-average pace, gaining 0.3% in
October.
The market impact of the October CPI report was accentuated by hawkish remarks from Fed Governor

Waller prior to the release. He expressed confidence in economic activity and the labour market while
showing significant concern over the strength and persistence of inflation, focusing on the passthrough
of energy and technology input inflation. His conclusion for policy was clear: "if, as I expect, the
monthly rate of inflation improves in October, I am leaning toward supporting an increase in our policy
rate of 25 basis points at the FOMC's December meeting"; but "if the October CPI inflation data out

later today are similar to the past two monthly readings—with core CPI inflation around 0.3 percent or
even higher—then a stronger policy move may be needed".
Regional Fed Presidents Goolsbee, Logan and Hammack also kept the focus on inflation risks and
stated or implied the FOMC needs to act. Market participants have responded to this week’s updates,
pricing a 25bp hike in for the December and January meetings, and a cumulative 3.5 hikes by
September. We believe this pricing overstates the need for policy tightening given core inflation ex

shelter remains relatively benign at 2.4% on a six-month annualised basis and the labour market is
balanced; but it will take time for price concerns to subside.
While we agree that the US economy is strong in aggregate, US households are under pressure from
the cost of living, and their investment decision making is constrained by borrowing costs. Highlighting

### Page 3

Related articles
this, US existing home sales fell 5.5% in October after a downwardly revised 1.0% gain in September,
leaving the level of sales below the cycle lows of 2009 and 2010. Our November Market Outlook will
provide a full update on our expectations for the US and the world when released on Westpac IQ this
afternoon.

## Document 333: EcoNews of 9 November 2026 (2026-11-09)

Source PDF: all report pdf/EcoNews of 9 November 2026 (2026-11-09).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-11-09
Pages: 2
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

The bank
for a changing
world
6
economic-research.bnpparibas.comEcoWeek 26.41 / 9 November 2026ECONEWS
Find out more in our scenario and forecasts
ADVANCED ECONOMIES
In brief. The week was dominated by large market swings triggered by
the US midterms results. In the end, energy prices end the week much
lower, bond yields somewhat lower and stock prices down in tech-heavy
markets and slightly up elsewhere, notably in Europe. Incoming data
confirmed a picture of resilient growth overall. The Bank of England

hiked its policy rate by 25 bps and signaled it could do more. Market
pressure on France eased somewhat.
EUROZONE
Energy pushes inflation up, but underlying pressures remain rela -
tively contained. Headline inflation rose to 4.0% y/y in October, its
highest in three years, strengthening the case for another ECB rate
increase in December. Energy remained the main driver, while core in-
flation rose by 2.6%. The ECB wage tracker points to negotiated wage

growth slowing to 2.3% by September 2027. Meanwhile, retail sales fell
0.7% m/m in September and the average interest rate on new business
loans increased 13bp to 3.90%.
To watch next week: September trade balance, Q3 GDP second esti -
mate and employment (Friday).
France
EVs boost activity, market pressure eases somewhat. After already
bolstering household consumption in Q3, new car registrations rose
7% y/y in October (+4% y/y YTD), driven by another surge in electric

car sales (+81% y/y in October; +78% y/y YTD). In parallel, manufac -
turing output rebounded by 0.5% m/m in September, supported by
the automotive sector (+7.5% m/m but -5.5% y/y) and capital goods
(+2.6% m/m and +4.8% y/y). However, industrial production fell by
0.5% m/m (and 1.3% y/y), as a result of a decline in electricity produc -
tion (-4.2% m/m). The composite October PMI was revised down by
1.8 points from the flash estimate to 46.6 (-4.5 m/m), dragged down by

services. This sharp downgrade likely reflects the hit to sentiment from
the October 17 riots; however, precedents suggest activity itself should
not be visibly impacted. Appropriately indifferent to the meandering
journey of the draft budget through Parliament, the OAT-bund spread
narrowed to 155 bps (-10 bps in one week) amid a broad-based decline
in government bond yields across advanced economies; Thursday’s
long-term debt auction, the last of the year, was well-subscribed. Stri-

ving to meet its deficit target of 5.4% of GDP in 2026, the government
announced EUR3.8bn of spending cuts to offset EUR3.7bn in additional
expenditure. The High Council for Public Finance nevertheless sees the
deficit reaching 5.5%. The 2027 budget will start being discussed by
the Senate from next week, in its original version, after the national
Assembly unanimously rejected its amended version that would have
led to a 5.9% deficit.
To watch next week: Q3 unemployment rate and the Banque de France

monthly business survey (Tuesday).
Germany
Industrial activity picks up. New orders rebounded 4.4% m/m in Sep -
tember and industrial production increased 0.7%, with the auto sec -
tor delivering particularly striking gains of 10.5% in orders and 17.1%
in production. Mechanical-engineering orders also recovered (+8.3%),
while pharmaceutical production increased by 8.0%. By contrast, large
orders in aerospace, shipbuilding, rail and defence fell by 10.4%, while

machinery and equipment production declined by 6.5%. October's com-
posite PMI was revised up to 53.4, signaling continued strong expan -
sion.
Italy
Services keep the economy above water as manufacturing slips back.
The composite PMI eased to 50.8 in October, its lowest since May.
Manufacturing returned to contraction at 49.4, its weakest reading
since January, while services strengthened to 52.0 and recorded a fifth
consecutive month of growth. Both sectors reported increasing input-

cost and output-price inflation.
UNITED KINGDOM
The BoE hikes and leaves the door open to more. The Bank of England
raised Bank Rate by 25bp to 4%, its first increase since August 2023, in a
more hawkish than expected 8-1 vote, with Catherine Mann favouring
a 50bp hike. The November Monetary Policy Report sees CPI inflation
peaking around 5% y/y by mid-2027 and upgraded Q4 2026 growth to
2.2%, while cutting the 2027 and 2028 growth projections. The com -

posite PMI meanwhile improved to 52.9, its highest since February,
with new business growing at its strongest pace in nine months and
private-sector employment increasing for the first time in 25 months.
To watch next week: Q3 GDP, business investment, industrial produc -
tion (Thursday).
UNITED STATES
Post election market jitters and a hot jobs report. The Democrats’
unexpectedly sweeping victory in the midterms (the largest wave
since 1974) initially led markets to rally on (i) relief that the President

recognised the results, and (ii) hopes of a swift end to the Iran war
(oil prices and bond yields down, stock prices up). But this was soon
followed by a sharp equity sell-off on 5 November, with the S&P 500
down 4.4% and the Nasdaq down 5.0%, erasing USD2.7 Trillion of mar-
ket capitalization in one day. Why? Because of the realization that vo -
ters strongly supported AI-restrictive measures on the ballot (46 out
of 47) and candidates — for Congress and Governor seats — in favour

of curtailing data centers buildout. By the week-end, however, senti -
ment had turned around again, helped in part by a much stronger than
expected jobs report: payrolls increased by 165,000 in October against
a 70,000 consensus, while the previous two months were revised hi -
gher and unemployment edged down to 4.1%. Average hourly earnings
rose 0.4% m/m, their strongest monthly pace since November 2025.
Manufacturing activity also strengthened, with the ISM rising to 55.5,

and the non-manufacturing index easing to a still buoyant 54.5.
To watch next week: October CPI (Tuesday); October PPI (Friday).
JAPAN
Wage growth accelerates. Scheduled contractual earnings increased
3.7% y/y in September and total wage growth accelerated to 4.6%,
helped by strong bonus payments. Real wage growth remained posi -
tive at 2.2% y/y.
EMERGING ECONOMIES
Portfolio outflows. According to IIF data, non-resident portfolio invest-
ment flows recorded USD33.8bn of outflows in October, the largest

monthly outflow since March and up from USD19.3bn in September.
Equities accounted for USD31.7bn, led by USD28.3bn from emerging
Asia, as doubts over AI-investment returns and profit-taking hit North
Asian markets particularly hard. Taiwan and South Korea suffered a
further sharp market correction in the first week of November.

### Page 2

7
economic-research.bnpparibas.comEcoWeek 26.41 / 9 November 2026
The bank
for a changing
world
Find out more in our scenario and forecasts
ASIA
PMI: expanding except for the Philippines. The ASEAN PMI rose to 53.1
in October from 52.1, pointing to faster expansion. India remained ro -
bust despite a slight decline (54.7). In Taiwan and South Korea, the
manufacturing PMIs declined but remained expansionary, at 54.9 and
51.3, respectively. Indonesia’s real GDP growth accelerated to 5.5% y/y

in Q3 2026 from 5.3% in Q2 marking its fastest pace since 2013.
China
The PBOC steps in as equity correction deepens. The central bank in -
creased its use of liquidity tools designed to support equity purchases,
including the special relending facility for share buybacks and stake in-
creases and a third round of the Securities, Funds and Insurance Com-
panies Swap Facility, for which 31 institutions applied for more than
RMB100bn. The measures follow heavy stock-market losses between

June and October. The PBOC also raised the reserve-requirement ratio
on foreign-currency deposits to 6% from 4%, effective 15 November, in
an effort to slow the renminbi's appreciation.
Weak domestic inflation pressure: In October, CPI inflation slowed to
0.7% y/y from 0.9% in September. Fuel prices increased by 20.2% y/y,
food prices fell -2.9% and core inflation slowed to 0.8% y/y from 1.0%.
Producer price inflation continued to rise (reaching 5.7% vs. 5.0% in

September), principally driven by higher energy and electronics prices.
To watch next week: External-trade data (Tuesday).
EMEA
Central Europe
PMIs expansionary except in Romania. Poland’s PMI hit the 50-mark
for the first time since April 2025 thanks to export orders. PMI was
also higher in Czech Rep. (55), supported by new orders, output and
employment. Hungary’s PMI fell marginally to 53.1.
Poland and Czech Republic central banks hold and hike respectively,

as expected.
Gulf
PMIs resilient except Qatar (deteriorating further to 46.3).
Saudi Arabia: Massive budget deficit in sight. Saudi Arabia recorded a
budget deficit of USD22.5bn in Q3, narrowing by 5% y/y. But over the
first nine months of 2026, budget deficit reached USD65.1bn, up from
USD48bn in 2025, despite a rather good revenue performance (+2.8%
total; +7.8% for non-oil revenues; -1.3% for oil revenues). The 8% rise in
spending in Q1-Q3 partly due to the war will make it difficult to meet

the 2026 revised target (deficit of 4.9% of GDP).
LATIN AMERICA
PMIs remain weak across the region, with only Colombia in expansion.
Brazil’s central bank pauses rate cuts, whereas Mexico’s central bank
cuts rates whilst signalling an upcoming pause. On 4 November, Bra -
zil’s central bank held its Selic rate at 13.75%, interrupting a series of
five consecutive cuts, amid rising inflation (4.6% y/y in September, up
from 4.2%), a sharp post-election real depreciation on fiscal concerns,

and higher energy prices. The central bank also revised up its 2027
inflation forecast to 4.7% (from 3.9%) and removed forward guidance
hinting at further cuts. Mexico’s central bank unexpectedly cut its po -
licy rate by 25bp to 6.25% on 5 November. This decision came despite
above-target inflation (3.5% against a 3% target), a peso depreciation
of more than 9% against USD since September, and relatively strong Q3
GDP growth (3.2% y/y). The central bank signaled that rates would now

remain on hold for an extended period.
COMMODITIES
Geopolitical relief delivers a dramatic energy-price reversal. Mar -
kets have priced in the reduced risk of short-term conflict escalation
through a sharp decline in prices. Beyond this, however, they lack di -
rection, wavering between positive signals (the resumption of indirect
negotiations between the belligerents) and less favorable ones (in -
creased vessel damage in the strait). Brent dropped 7.6% over the week

to USD96.8/bbl as markets priced a reduced risk of near-term conflict
escalation. European gas prices fell almost in lockstep, declining 7.7%
to EUR87.6/MWh. US commercial crude inventories continued to de -
cline, although more modestly than over the preceding four weeks.
Diesel crack spread’s reaction to US elections has been much more
limited than for crude. EU gas storage ended the refilling season at 75%
of capacity (vs. 80% goal).
y/y = year-on-year

m/m = month-on-month
bp = basis point
pp = percentage point
mb = million barrels
MBtu = Million British Thermal Units

Economics | 3

### Page 4

Monthly Economics
Sector Analysis
Personal Consumption Expenditures (PCE)
• We did not materially alter our real PCE forecast, and expect consumer spending growth to
continue.
Consumer spending has held up remarkably well this year despite higher gasoline costs weighing on
household purchasing power. While we do not expect the recent above-trend near-4% annualized
pace of real consumption growth to be sustained, we see consumers continuing to spend at a healthy

clip into next year. A firmer income backdrop revealed by recent revisions, continued support from
household wealth and an expected recovery in purchasing power as inflation slows should keep
consumption on solid footing. We expect real consumption growth to remain north of 2% in the final
quarter of the year and roughly match this year’s average pace in 2027.
Investment: Equipment, Intellectual Property Products, and Inventories
• A stronger-than-expected capex outturn in Q3 has revealed a slightly firmer trend headed into

next year.
Business investment continues to be driven by the high-tech buildout, with real equipment spending
stronger than expected in Q3 and the latest data suggest a firmer trend. We have not materially
changed our outlook following the Democratic sweep in the midterms. Our forecast already assumes
some moderation in high-tech investment next year and a smaller contribution to GDP growth as the
buildout matures. Beyond high-tech, we expect only a modest broadening in capex spending over the

coming year.
Source: U.S. Department of Commerce and Wells Fargo Economics
Source: U.S. Department of Commerce and Wells Fargo Economics
Investment: Residential
• We have not made major changes to our outlook for residential investment. High mortgage rates
and challenging affordability conditions look set to continue pressuring home sales and new single-
family construction.
Not much has changed in the way of residential investment. Mortgage rates have come in somewhat,

but are still elevated above 7.0%. Building permits are stuck in a low gear, with single-family projects
continuing to moderate against a backdrop of weak buyer demand and elevated inventories.
Multifamily permits have picked up modestly, reflecting renewed strength in apartment demand.
Modestly lower mortgage rates and stabilizing apartment market conditions should help put a floor
beneath residential investment and bring about modest growth over the course of next year.

Investment: Nonresidential Structures
• Structures investment is likely to maintain healthy momentum amid robust data center, power, and
related nonresidential construction.
4 | Economics

### Page 5

Economic Outlook: November 2026 Economics
Structures investment accelerated notably in Q3. The bulk of this momentum can be traced to data
centers and power, however, activity also appears to be broadening out. As examples, private spending
on communication and transportation infrastructure have perked up and manufacturing construction
appears to be stabilizing on increased semiconductor investment. The downturn in commercial
construction has also softened as data centers shore up demand for warehousing. Although new

regulations may soften the pace of data center starts, there are more than enough projects already in
the pipeline to support healthy structures investment through next year.

Net Exports & Trade
• Net exports should remain a fairly persistent drag on U.S. real GDP growth amid continued
imports.
A surge in imports, particularly of high-tech goods like computers, caused net exports to subtract

more than 3 percentage points from Q3 GDP growth. We expect trade to remain a drag in Q4, though
with little data in hand, that largely reflects where trade flows ended last quarter and an assumption
of another sizable gain in imports. Looking ahead, continued strength in high-tech investment should
keep import growth elevated and the trade deficit wide, leaving net exports a persistent drag on U.S.
growth. Exports remain more volatile, but imports are increasingly the bigger driver of the trade story.

Labor Market
• Labor demand has improved modestly in recent months. We now expect payroll growth to average
65K per month in 2027, and the unemployment rate to hold steady at 4.1% over the next four
quarters.
The October jobs report reinforced our view that the labor market remains solid. The pickup in job
growth this year aligns with purchasing manager surveys that point to expanding employment across
the goods and services sectors. At the same time, the historically low trends in initial jobless claims and

the layoff rate suggest employers broadly remain reluctant to shed workers. Structurally, firms appear
focused on retaining existing staff and investing heavily in AI adoption while remaining selective about
adding to head count—a dynamic that should keep the low-hire, low-fire environment largely intact.
We expect payroll growth to average around 65K per month in 2027, roughly in line with our estimate
of breakeven job growth. That should keep the unemployment rate steady at 4.1% through next year

and labor market conditions generally consistent with full employment.
Inflation
• We look for the core PCE deflator to rise 3.3% on a Q4-over-Q4 basis in 2026 before slowing to
2.6% in 2027.
The October inflation report reinforced the persistence of price pressures, with upside surprises
emerging across a range of categories. Although gasoline and diesel prices have receded in recent
Economics | 5

### Page 6

Monthly Economics
weeks, earlier increases in transportation costs are still working their way through supply chains. Food
inflation picked up in October and looks poised to strengthen further. The outlook for energy prices
remains highly uncertain and represents the largest swing factor for inflation, in our view. Within the
core, we continue to expect goods inflation to moderate in 2027 as tariff-related pressures fade
further and demand tied to the AI buildout cools. Services inflation, however, is likely to prove stickier.

Health care services prices have been running hot and should continue to face upward pressure from
the aging population, and primary shelter inflation looks to be stabilizing. Taken together, we expect
inflation to remain above the Fed's 2% target through next year and look for the core PCE deflator to
end 2027 at 2.6% on a Q4-over-Q4 basis.

Fiscal Policy
• The lame duck session of Congress probably will not yield a budget reconciliation bill or a debt

ceiling resolution. The December 11 government funding deadline probably will be punted to Q1/
early Q2 via another continuing resolution.
• We project a federal budget deficit of $2.0 trillion for FY 2027, similar to FY 2026 in dollar terms
but a bit smaller as a share of GDP (~6%).
Assuming that Congress does not increase or suspend the debt ceiling in the next six weeks, the
incoming Democratic Congress will need to negotiate a resolution with President Trump in 2027.

Right now, it looks like Treasury will hit the debt ceiling around early March. At that point, Treasury can
lean on extraordinary measures and its cash on hand to last for about 7-8 months, putting the debt
ceiling "X date" somewhere around early Q4. We suspect the debt ceiling/annual budget negotiations
will include a lift to defense and nondefense discretionary government spending for FY 2027/2028,
but we doubt the bump will be enough to meaningfully move our economic growth or deficit forecasts.

For now, our working assumption is that divided government will not produce any major new fiscal
expansions/contractions over the next two years, leaving the budget deficit picture as status quo until
after the 2028 election.
Monetary Policy & Interest Rates
• Our baseline forecast is for another 25 bp rate hike to the fed funds rate in December, followed by
an extended hold from the FOMC through 2027. The risks are tilted toward 1-2 more hikes early

next year.
• Our forecast for the 10-year Treasury yield at year-end 2027 is 4.85%.
The recent inflation data seem to make a hike in December a lock so long as financial conditions don't
completely implode in the coming weeks. The core CPI came in about 8 bps above consensus at 0.3%
for October, and a 0.4% on core PCE is plausible, depending on how the PPI data shake out. This should
put the FOMC's hawks back in the driver's seat after the October skip.

Economic Outlook: November 2026 Economics
Past December, the outlook will be highly dependent on future inflation data. Our expectation is that
core inflation will cool gradually in 2027, and we still do not see clear signs of demand-side inflation in
the data. But hiking cycles have an inertial component to them, and the recent inflation data reset the
clock to zero on the Fed seeing several good inflation prints in a row. This creates some modest upside

risk to our base case for the fed funds rate next year.

International Developments
• Global growth has proven more resilient, with our 2026 growth forecast revised higher and a
modest improvement in the underlying 2027 outlook.
• Our 2026 inflation forecast has moved slightly lower, while our 2027 forecast has moved higher,
with inflationary risks likely to persist into 2027.
• The monetary policy bias remains toward extended tightening across advanced economies.

Emerging market policy paths remain differentiated, but have also shifted in a more hawkish
direction, with some central banks bringing forward rate hikes and others having less room to ease
in 2027.
The balance of risks to global inflation remains tilted to the upside. Elevated geopolitical uncertainty,
resilient activity and persistent energy and supply-chain pressures increase the risk that inflation
remains higher for longer. Although the direct effects of supply shocks may prove temporary, central

banks are increasingly focused on delayed pass-through, persistent services inflation and potential
second-round effects on wages, prices and inflation expectations. Higher government bond yields are
meanwhile keeping financial conditions tight. The result is a shift toward more extended tightening,
with several rate hikes moving further into the forecast horizon and less room for easing in 2027.
Across advanced economies, the tightening cycle is increasingly extending into 2027. Our European

Central Bank outlook is unchanged, with a 25 bps hike in December still our base case, although
tighter financial conditions raise the risk of a later move. In the U.K., our November Bank of England
hike was realized, and we now expect another 25 bps hike in Q1-2027, reflecting renewed inflation
pressures associated with the Ofgem price-cap reset.
Elsewhere, the policy outlook has turned more hawkish in Canada. Having previously flagged upside
risks to our year-end call, we now expect the Bank of Canada to hike at consecutive meetings, bringing

the policy rate to 3.00% by Q1-2027. In Australia, the Reserve Bank of Australia raised the Cash Rate
again in November. After five hikes, we think policymakers have room to assess how tighter policy
feeds through the economy, although risks remain skewed toward another increase if inflation stays
elevated or activity proves stronger than expected. Japan is unchanged: we continue to expect the
Bank of Japan to hike in December and again in 2027, bringing the terminal rate to 1.75%.

Emerging market policy paths remain differentiated but have also shifted more hawkishly. In India,
resilient growth and rising inflation have led us to bring forward our second Reserve Bank of India

hike from Q1-2027 to December 2026. Brazil is moving toward higher-for-longer policy, with
persistent inflation raising the bar for additional easing; we now expect cuts to resume in Q2 rather
than Q1-2027 and see less easing overall next year. In Mexico, Banxico's cut to 6.25% was more easing
than we expected, and with inflation risks still tilted to the upside, we now expect rates to remain on
hold. China remains the exception: continued targeted policy support and resilient exports should

support growth, prompting most upward revisions to our 2026 and 2027 growth forecasts, while
greater scope for easing likely emerges in 2027.

Reserve's Christopher Waller mentioned a 50bp hike and core CPI
overshot on 10 November, while other Fed speakers have done little
to quell the hawkish repricing. We’d be more bearish on the dollar if
markets had jumped to pricing a 50bp move in December, which
seems unlikely. Instead, the Fed funds futures curve favours back-
to-back 25bp increases in December and January. That looks not
only more realistic but, crucially, considerably harder for moderate

FOMC members to push back against than an imminent 50bp
move.
We therefore do not see this week’s long list of Fed speakers as a
signiﬁcant downside risk for the dollar. Chair Kevin Warsh speaks
today, but he’s been wary of oﬀering guidance, especially outside
of meetings, so we wouldn't be surprised to get very little from his
remarks. The minutes from the October FOMC meeting are due on
Wednesday. The recent hawkish turn in Fedspeak and hotter core

CPI have probably raised the bar for a market-impactful surprise.
The larger risk of a USD correction comes from US equities, which
have shown great potential to drive dovish repricing. The AI trade
faces two major tests this week: Nvidia reports quarterly earnings
tomorrow, while Anthropic is expected to make its stock market
debut on Thursday. So far, tech stocks have held up remarkably
well despite Nvidia’s idiosyncratic selloﬀ, and futures currently point

to a modestly positive start to the week.
Today’s Empire Manufacturing index is one of the few notable data
inputs this week. Given the size of the front-end rates move and the
arguably high bar for further hawkish surprises, we tend to favour
dollar stabilisation rather than another leg higher. But for now, we
think markets will tend to fade dollar corrections.

CEE: Vain wait for sustainable relief

^New 
Transitioning to mid-cycle 
• We now favour a more balanced stance between stocks and bonds. Near-
term event risks and high earnings expectations prompt us to trim equities to a 
Core holding. The US and Asia ex-Japan (AxJ) remain our preferred regions. 
Within AxJ, we upgrade India to Overweight. 
• Higher bond yields are improving the outlook for future returns and offering 
increasingly attractive income opportunities. We are Overweight Emerging 
Market (EM) USD bonds and favour corporate over government bonds, but limit 
duration to 3-7 years. 
• We remain Overweight gold. Prices are likely to grind higher as near-term 
headwinds from higher bond yields are offset by EM central bank demand. 
Great expectations 
Individual risk events, high earnings expectations and a higher cost of capital 
point to a more moderate return environment ahead. In the very near term, 
financial markets must navigate key event risks, such as (i) the US mid-term elections, 
which have historically been associated with elevated market volatility and (ii) renewed 
geopolitical tensions, with several reports suggesting the US is considering renewed 
strikes on Iran. These concerns have pushed the Brent oil price well above USD 
110/bbl despite significant ongoing crude oil flows through the Strait of Hormuz.  
Higher bond yields combined with high earnings expectations raise the risk of 
an expectations reset. The US Q3 earnings season is attracting significant attention 
given high earnings expectations, particularly for the AI sector. However, higher US 
bond yields may start to bite as debt-financed capex takes on a greater share of total 
capex over the coming year. This, combined with already-high expectations, raises the 
risk of disappointment and a near-term repricing of expected earnings growth pace.  
Despite the current challenges, healthy growth continues to underpin the 
economic backdrop. The US economic outlook remains well supported, and the 
absolute level of year-ahead earnings expectations is high. We do not expect Fed rate 
hikes to result in overly restrictive policy. Our caution, thus, stems from the risk of an 
expectations repricing rather than an outright economic slowdown.

1 Investment strategy and key themes

Steve Brice

Manpreet Gill

Raymond Cheng, CFA, CPA

Fig. 1 Our quant model score has fallen to levels

A more balanced stock-bond mix 
We trim global equities to a Core holding, leaving us 
Neutral on both equities and bonds and Overweight gold. 
The concerns laid out earlier point to near-term volatility risks 
and a somewhat lower long-term growth trajectory for 
equities. Our quant model also reflects this cautious outlook, 
having downgraded equities to a level usually associated with 
flat-to-modestly-negative returns. 
From a relative perspective, though, the higher yields on 
offer from bonds also raise the bar by presenting an 
increasingly compelling return outlook after several 
years of very low yields. We believe this argues for portfolios 
that are increasingly balanced between equities and bonds. 
Ways to diversify equity exposure 
We continue to favour global equity market 
diversification, with a preference for US and AxJ equities. 
We remain Overweight US and AxJ equities, expecting them 
to outperform other major regions. Earnings growth remains 
the main driver of this view – while growth rates could 
decelerate somewhat, we still expect them to remain higher 
than those of other major regions.  
Japan and Euro area equities remain attractive as 
regional diversifiers. Our positive view on both markets’ 
financial sectors supports our Core holding view on them.   
Within Asia, we are Overweight Taiwan and India. Indian 
equities have lagged the region this year, in part due to limited 
listed AI-related exposure. However, we believe this is 
increasingly likely to become an advantage as investors seek 
greater diversification away from excessive concentration in 
the AI theme. This comes at a time when the economic and 
earnings growth outlook for Indian equities is starting to 
improve. We retain a more Neutral view on Chinese equities 
amid a still-challenging growth outlook. 
We also favour greater sector diversification. In the US, 
we favour diversifying across our Overweight sectors of 
technology, financials, materials and healthcare. We also 
favour financials in Japan and the Euro area.  
Changes to our equity opportunistic ideas also reflect a 
more defensive stance. In recent weeks, we have closed our 
global semiconductors, MSCI Taiwan, US communication 
services and global power & electrification ideas and have 
opened new ideas on US healthcare and MSCI Switzerland. 
Riding the yield train 
We believe bond markets offer an attractive opportunity 
to lock in yields. The 10-year US government bond yield is 
now in a range last witnessed in 2007. While the process of 
rising yields results in lower bond prices – as painfully 
experienced in recent months – the yield level itself starts to 
significantly improve the outlook for future returns. 
Favour corporate over government bonds, but check 
your duration. Still-high credit quality means that we continue 
to favour taking on credit risk to earn a higher yield (EM USD 
bonds are preferred, but we also see investment-grade (IG) 
and high-yield (HY) bonds as Core holdings). However, 
limiting duration to 3-7 years remains key to avoiding 
excessive exposure to any inflation surprises. 
Changes to our bond opportunistic ideas also reflect a 
more balanced stance. We close our EU bank contingent 
convertible bonds (CoCos2 – please refer page 27) and US 
utilities corporate hybrids ideas. We also close our 
opportunistic idea on US 10-year government bonds following 
the recent pullback in yields, locking in gains. 
Alternatives and gold to remain relevant 
Alternatives are an effective tool for portfolio risk 
management. Hedge funds and private markets provide a 
differentiated return stream, while rotating into alternatives 
helps reduce equity beta exposure. We examine downside 
capture and how beta is reduced via hedge fund sub-
strategies and private markets in our asset allocation section.  
Gold remains a key Overweight. We trim our three- and 12-
month forecasts modestly, but see gold forming a strong base 
above USD 4,000/oz. Rising bond yields and the USD were 
recent headwinds, but we expect a softer USD and continued 
EM central bank demand to result in 12-month gains.

accessible to most investors, while the Foundation+ model includes allocations to private assets that may be accessible to 
investors in some jurisdictions, but not others.

### Page 7

Global Market Outlook 7

Key themes

Core scenario (soft landing, 45% probability): An economic soft landing remains our core scenario, with the global economy 
overcoming this year’s oil price shock thanks to a surge in AI investment in the US and Asia. Record wealth generation driven 
by the stock market rally is supporting US consumption. Fiscal easing in the US, Germany and Japan is supporting growth. 
Meanwhile, inflation has reaccelerated due to a rebound in fuel prices as the Middle East conflict drags on. Against this backdrop, 
the Fed, ECB and BoJ all hiked rates in the same month (in September) for the first time. Although all three subsequently paused 
in October, we expect the Fed to hike twice more, the ECB once more and the BoJ thrice more by June 2027. Nevertheless, we 
expect the energy and tariff effects on inflation to fade by H2 2027, enabling the Fed and the ECB to cut rates by Q3 2027. 
Meanwhile, China is likely to target credit easing and accelerate fiscal spending in H1 2027 to revive domestic growth.  
Upside risk (no landing, 30% probability): We maintain the probability at 30% as AI-driven investment growth continues. 
There is a reasonable chance that lower oil prices next year, the AI-fuelled wealth boom and fiscal easing in Germany and Japan 
will boost ‘animal spirits’. A potential global defence spending boom, led by the US and Europe, could also lift global growth. 
Downside risk (25% probability): We raised the risk of a recession to 15%, especially if central banks are forced to accelerate 
the pace of rate hikes to douse rising inflation. A stock market downturn negating the wealth effect or a bond sell-off due to fiscal 
concerns are other tail risks. We cut stagflation probability to 10% as we see low risk of oil prices sustaining above USD 120/bbl.

Source: Bloomberg, Standard Chartered

Policy rates watch  
We expect the Fed to hike twice more by Q2 2027, then 
ease in H2 2027, as AI-led expansion continues. The US 
economy remains firmly in expansion. In Q3, annualised GDP 
growth accelerated to 3.4%, led by AI investments and 
consumption. In October, the composite Purchasing 
Managers’ Index (PMI) remained strong at 57.6. Since our last 
monthly report, the consensus estimate for 2026 GDP growth 
has been nudged up to 2.3% from 2.1%, and the 2027 
estimate has risen to 2.2%. That matches the Fed’s upgraded 
growth projection of 2.3% for 2026, but remains below the 
Fed’s 2.4% estimate for 2027. AI investment, fiscal support 
and household wealth are the main growth drivers, although 
consumer activity lags business spending. Higher bond yields 
and elevated oil prices are headwinds, but recession risks 
look contained. Our core scenario remains a ‘soft landing’, at 
45%, with a meaningful 30% chance of a ‘no-landing’ upside. 
Rising gasoline and diesel prices pushed US core personal 
consumption expenditures (PCE) inflation to 0.3% m/m and 
3.1% y/y, matching estimates. Consensus sees core PCE 
inflation slowing from 3.2% in Q4 2026 to 2.4% by Q4 2027 
as energy and tariff effects fade. Underlying price momentum 
is softening, and recent PCE methodology revisions have 
lowered core inflation. However, fuel costs passed on to wider 
consumers and AI-driven chip shortages remain upside risks. 
In October, the Fed held rates at 4.0%, after hiking in 
September for the first time since 2023. We expect the Fed to 
hike rates by 25bps in December and 25bps more in H1 2027 
due to elevated near-term energy inflation. It is likely to follow 
with a 25bps cut to 4.25% in Q3 2027 as disinflation returns. 
This aligns with the Fed’s projected policy path, although it 
implies a more dovish trajectory than money markets, which 
are pricing another 56bps of hikes over the next 12 months. 
Political uncertainty after the mid-term election is a key risk.

6 Macro overview – at a glance
